# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Independent relation-map review — round 14

Reviewed **`73ee310d970c8c3b4886bbbdfd6d88026d403c28`**. **Changes required: two P1 findings**, one regression introduced by the latest patch and one inherited refusal bypass. `3HVINBLASTINE` and `89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT` pass every refusal stage and draw their originator molecules. The main M13 DARATUMUMAB reproductions are fixed; its supplementary `A33124I` example is still **NOT FIXED**.

No tracked file was edited. All new scripts, temporary files and results are under `audit_evidence_claims/m14/`. Existing m14 brief/log files were not edited by the audit. Known accepted over-refusals are retained as nonblockers. The verdict rests on reproduced originator leaks, not those over-refusals or performance observations.

## Verification and scope

- **341 passed in 30.33 s**, exit 0, using the requested `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; bytecode disabled and temporary paths confined to m14. [Output](root_checks/pytest.txt), [execution and hashes](root_checks/pytest_summary.json).
- Cumulative original HTTP reproducers were rerun, including the original m1 audit, m2b-m10 additions, exact m11 cases, and m12/m13 confirmations. All batches exit 0. Source scripts were reused with output-path/owned-port adaptations; original case inputs were retained. [Root manifest](reuse_manifest_root.json), [main batches](root_checks/execution_all.json), [m6](root_checks/execution_m6_http.json), [remaining](root_checks/execution_remaining.json), [m12/m13](root_checks/execution_rounds12_13.json).
- **55 reused name scripts / 45 executed leaf reproductions**, all exit 0, and **64 individual historical disposition rows** below. Replays include the **7,511-key** alias sweep and the **1,648-question** frozen counterfactual. Zero new lone-alias losses; `rad001c` alone recovered. The frozen comparison remains **68 identical / 1,556 metadata-only / 24 semantic / zero errors**. These comparisons swap methods against the same current data; they are not reconstructed historic deployments. [Name verification](names/FINDINGS.md), [manifest](names/reuse_manifest.json), [execution checks](names/worker_final_checks.json).

[Oracle](db_ui/db_validate.json), [DB](db_ui/db_assertions.json), [UI](db_ui/ui_assertions.json), [fixture](db_ui/self_loop_unknown.json).

Original QA evidence counts remain **12, 12, 11, 1, 12, 0, 0**. [Comparisons](db_ui/demo_previous_comparison.json), [HTTP](db_ui/demo_http.json), [callbacks](db_ui/ui_m3_checks.json).
- Attack matrices contain **97 current/HEAD^ direct comparison cases** and **308 final HTTP responses**, all HTTP 200. The root independently confirmed 24 cases through **96 HTTP requests**, and a separate reviewer traced both failures through `_marked` and the complete linker. [Attack execution](attacks/execution.json), [stages](attacks/edge_results.json), [independent HTTP](root_checks/independent_m14_http.json), [lifecycle](root_checks/independent_m14_lifecycle.json), [independent adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md).

UI checks execute the actual page JavaScript in a Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch stubs. They verify generated markup and the exercised state/callback paths, not browser painting or browser-executed XSS. HTTP uses unmodified `src/app.py` or its real handler with isolated in-memory fixtures. No audit used port 8765 or called a model. Drug identity evidence comes from the repository dictionary, canonical keys and refused controls; no external pharmacology assumption is needed.

The successful raw-alias sweep does not cover every separator-removal/case variant of those aliases. It therefore does not contradict the transformed-alias regression below.

## M14-B1 — a consumed one-letter-isotope alias bypasses the case-marked check (regression)

**Exact HTTP:** `POST /api/explore`, JSON `{"text":"3HVINBLASTINE"}`. HTTP 200 draws **VINBLASTINE / CHEMBL159**, matched alias **`[3h]-vinblastine`**, with **5 live trials**, and no refusal. `Which trials evaluate 3HVINBLASTINE?` and `Who sponsors trials of 3HVINBLASTINE?` each return **5 originator evidence rows**. `3HVinblastine`, `3Hvinblastine`, and `3hvinblastine` do the same. With `3Hvinblastine, osimertinib`, the wrong first centre is still VINBLASTINE. [Full stages and previous comparison](attacks/edge_results.json), [HTTP](attacks/edge_http.json).

**Control and identity:** `[3h]-vinblastine` and `[3H]vinblastine` are refused, with zero trial/sponsor evidence. Their canonical key is the same **`3hvinblastine`** as the accepted input, uniquely naming the stored tracer alias of VINBLASTINE. Bare `vinblastine` remains available. For the deliberately case-marked input **`3HVINBLASTINE`**, `_isotope_edge` returns **true**. Thus this finding does not depend on reading lower-case chemistry as an isotope. [Dictionary, helper result, key checks](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json).

The HEAD^ query module refuses all four glued spellings and selects the separate OSIMERTINIB in the comma control. This is a **current-patch regression**, established with the same current app, normalization/rules, and database and only `query.py` replaced in memory. It is not a reconstructed old deployment. [Version-labelled direct results](attacks/edge_results.json).

**Cause:** positive matching consumes the complete alias (`src/query.py:450-470`). The new helper is called only on **outside neighbouring words** (`src/query.py:317-320`), never on the consumed name's `own` words. The inside scan (`src/query.py:282-297,321-323`) recognizes whole isotope labels but cannot split `3HVINBLASTINE`. The revised key fallback excludes one-letter elements and starts its prefix-length loop at 3 (`src/query.py:327-337`), although `3H` has length 2. The previous key fallback refused this canonical alias. The comment saying that one-letter elements are left to the case-marked check is ineffective for a consumed alias.

**Required outcome:** preserve refusal for a known tracer alias when its isotope separator is removed, including the case-marked form the new helper already recognizes, without broadening lower-case dose/chemistry interpretation.

## M14-B2 — a two-letter-isotope alias with a trailing PET/CT marker still maps its originator (inherited)

**Exact HTTP:** `POST /api/explore`, JSON `{"text":"89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT"}`. HTTP 200 draws **PANITUMUMAB / CHEMBL1201827**, matched alias **`89zr-panitumumab-pet/ct`**, with **8 live trials**. Trial and sponsor QA return **8 / 7 originator evidence rows**. There is no Refused entity or refusal notice. [HTTP](attacks/edge_http.json), [stages](attacks/edge_results.json).

**Minimal control:** **`89Zr-panitumumab-PET-CT`** refuses and yields zero QA evidence. Removing only the hyphen between the isotope and the drug causes the leak. Both strings have canonical key **`89zrpanitumumabpetct`**, uniquely pointing to the same stored AACT alias. `89Zr panitumumab PET CT` also refuses; bare `panitumumab` still works. Fully glued `89ZrpanitumumabPETCT`, lower-case `89zrpanitumumabpetct`, and `89Zrpanitumumab PET CT` leak too. Appending `, osimertinib` does not prevent the wrong PANITUMUMAB centre. [Dictionary evidence](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json), [compact matrix](attacks/edge_summary.json).

**Cause:** the isotope is fused inside a consumed alias, so it is neither a whole isotope word nor an outside neighbour (`src/query.py:282-323`). `_isotope_edge('89Zrpanitumumab')` is true, but this helper is never applied to that own word. The final fallback isolates `89zr` but requires the remaining key **to end with** a same-drug key of at least five characters (`src/query.py:329-334`). `panitumumabpetct` does not end in any such key: the stored imaging marker follows the drug name. Allowing arbitrary text only **between** the isotope and a terminal same-drug name does not protect this actual stored tracer alias. The HEAD^ query module has the same leak, so this is inherited, not a newly introduced regression.

**Required outcome:** normalization of a stored refused tracer alias must preserve its refused identity even when the alias includes a trailing imaging marker.

## Every stage for both blockers

| Stage | `3HVINBLASTINE` | `89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT` |
|---|---|---|
| Raw intervention resolver | ALIAS / CHEMBL159 | ALIAS / CHEMBL1201827 |
| `_qualified` | false | false |
| Resolver veto | `{}` | `{}` |
| Item linker | Drug; no Refused | Drug; no Refused |
| Whole-name shortcut | `None` | `None` |
| Public linker | VINBLASTINE; no Refused | PANITUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Map centre / live trial count | CHEMBL159 / 5 | CHEMBL1201827 / 8 |
| Qualified trial / sponsor QA rows | 5 / 5 | 8 / 7 |

The raw tracer predicate expects a boundary after the element (`src/textrules.py:303-314`), so the normalized raw resolver also misses the guard (`src/normalize.py:738-746`). `Engine.explore` selects the surviving first positive hit (`src/app.py:88-95`). These are not cases where the map ignores an already-recorded refusal; **all refusal paths miss**. The relationships shown may be valid for the originator but are attached to a wrongly selected centre.

Both findings were independently reproduced over the unmodified CLI server. The root responses contain the same centre IDs and **5/5** or **8/7** qualified QA evidence counts; the separated controls refuse. The second reviewer recorded `_marked` returning `None` for both current candidates. [Root confirmation](root_checks/independent_m14_http.json), [separate stage traces](db_ui/adjudication_tracer.json). No repair was implemented in this audit.

## Supplementary cases and accepted over-refusals

The explicitly accepted M5-S2, `40-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)-rapamycin`, `42-o-(2-hydroxyethyl)rapamycin`, `Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate`, `9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin`, `il-6ra` and `TAK-659F` remain **NOT FIXED / known over-refusals**, not blockers. In contrast, M12/M13 `rad001c` and `RAD001C` are now **FIXED**: EVEROLIMUS / CHEMBL1908360, nine live trials. [Fresh alias comparisons](names/new_alias_regression.json), [root HTTP](root_checks/independent_m13_http.json).

M13's supplementary **`A33124I` remains NOT FIXED**: it still maps ADAKITUG / CHEMBL5314551, while `A33 124i` refuses. Both keys are `a33124i`. This has zero trial/sponsor QA evidence but still draws the originator; it is a residual previously reported example, not a third newly counted finding. The complete M13 finding family therefore cannot be called fully repaired. [Exact previous/current stages](attacks/fresh_results.json), [root HTTP](root_checks/independent_m13_http.json).

`99MTc trastuzumab` and `[99MTc] trastuzumab` remain **FIXED**. The earlier descriptive-distance isotope and punctuated lexical-qualifier cases remain **NOT FIXED** where recorded, including `trastuzumab labelled with 89Zr`, `89Zr labeled trastuzumab`, `b.i.o.similar bevacizumab` and `lip.osomal doxorubicin`; they are retained as supplementary observations rather than newly counted findings. `Lutetium (177L.u) dotatate` remains **FIXED** (its historical centre was already isotope-labelled). [Original small replay](root_checks/fresh_probe.json), [reused 41-case matrix](attacks/focused_results.json), [attack assessment](attacks/FINDINGS.md).

Additional neighbouring-word casing boundary: `89ZRDFO trastuzumab` and `89zrDFO trastuzumab` draw TRASTUZUMAB, while `89ZrDFO trastuzumab` refuses. The helper requires exact upper/lower casing for two-letter elements too (`src/query.py:133-134`). This is recorded without inflating the two stored-alias findings. [Stage evidence](attacks/extra_neighbors.json).

The exact controls `13-cis retinoic acid`, `BKM-120NX`, `osimertinib 80 mg`, `paclitaxel 2 cycles` and `cisplatin 75 mg/m2 day 1` still select their intended Drugs. [Independent HTTP](root_checks/independent_m14_http.json).

## Cost and performance reproductions

All nine inherited cost scripts exited 0, sequentially after the other reviewers finished their CPU/HTTP workloads. Unrelated host activity is uncontrolled. Timings are observations, not service-level guarantees or proof of global complexity. Historical comparisons substitute only `query.py` against the same current remaining modules and database. [Execution](cost/execution_isolated.json), [source provenance](reuse_manifest_root.json).

| Historical reproduction / exact workload | Current observation and disposition |
|---|---|

`engine.lock` serializes engine operations (`src/app.py:247-263`), so costly legal check-answer requests delay maps. The 3,999/4,000-character stresses are direct-link or **answer-field** cases; `/api/explore` correctly rejects text over 500 characters. Limits precede NFKC (`src/app.py:239-243,260`; `src/query.py:116-117,190-192`). No deadlock or invented graph value was observed. These costs remain should-fix items; they do not determine the refusal verdict. No model cost was incurred.

## Standards and specification

**Standards axis: zero documented violations found.** The application/UI diff from `bfcb83d` and latest query/test patch were inspected. No tracked repository AGENTS.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, CODING_STANDARDS.md or issue-tracker instructions were found. User-provided evidence-based, m14-only and no-tracked-edit requirements govern. KOA Studio / KhOpenApi original-versus-modified-code comparison applies when changing application code; none was changed. [Standards record](db_ui/standards_review.json).

**Specification axis: two P1 findings**, M14-B1 (regression) and M14-B2 (inherited). The main M13 repair works, but every refusal stage misses these stored-tracer variants. Correct edges around an incorrectly chosen originator do not meet the approval bar. The residual M13 `A33124I` example also remains unresolved. [Independent specification assessment](attacks/FINDINGS.md), [separate adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md).

README app descriptions remain accurate (`README.md:566`, `README.ko.md:387`). The nearby test-count comments still say 317: **NOT FIXED**, a documentation-only should-fix; the actual suite has 341 passing tests. [Source assessment](db_ui/FINDINGS.md).

## Integrity and reproduction

`Engine.ask` sets `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`); explore follows SELECT paths. Mutation fixtures use in-memory database copies. The app does not globally enforce a read-only SQLite handle, so the no-write result rests on source inspection, executed paths and byte equality rather than a database-permission guarantee.

Final checks compare **4,632 tracked-file SHA-256 hashes**, HEAD, tracked/staged diffs, database bytes, report links and owned-port cleanup. [Initial snapshot](integrity_before.json), [final verification](integrity_final.json). Database SHA-256: `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`.

Run from the repository root with bytecode disabled and TMP/TEMP/TMPDIR under the corresponding m14 family. Run shared-port suites sequentially and benchmarks after other suites. The manifests preserve original sources and adaptations; do not overwrite the initial integrity snapshot.

```text
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m14/root_checks/run_all.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m14/root_checks/run_rounds12_13.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m14/root_checks/independent_m14_http.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m14/names/run_round9.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m14/names/run_additional.py
```

"""Preserve tracked bytes; adapt historical audit scripts, never their evidence."""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib, json, subprocess

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
tracked = subprocess.check_output(['git', 'ls-files', '-z'], cwd=ROOT).decode('utf8').split('\0')
initial = {
    'head': subprocess.check_output(['git', 'rev-parse', 'HEAD'], cwd=ROOT).decode().strip(),
    'tracked_sha256': {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in tracked if p},
    'status': subprocess.check_output(['git', 'status', '--porcelain'], cwd=ROOT).decode(),
    'db_sha256': hashlib.sha256((ROOT / 'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
}
(OUT / 'integrity_before.json').write_text(json.dumps(initial, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
manifest = []
for sub in ['root_checks', 'cost']:
    dest = OUT / sub
    (dest / 'tmp').mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    for src in sorted((ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m12' / sub).glob('*.py')):
        if src.name in ('setup.py', 'build_report.py'):
            continue
        code = src.read_text(encoding='utf8')
        changes = []
        for old, new in [('8917', '19400'), ('8918', '19401'), ('8919', '19402'), ('8914', '19403')]:
            if old in code:
                code = code.replace(old, new)
                changes.append(f'port {old} -> {new}')
        assert 'audit_evidence_claims/m12/' not in code, src
        (dest / src.name).write_text(code, encoding='utf8')
        manifest.append({'source': src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(), 'copy': (dest/src.name).relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
                         'sha256': hashlib.sha256(src.read_bytes()).hexdigest(), 'changes': changes,
                         'output_root': '__file__ relative m13; historical inputs unchanged'})
(OUT / 'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({'tracked': len(initial['tracked_sha256']), 'scripts': len(manifest), 'head': initial['head']}))

### run_replays.py
"""Execute inherited reproducers, owning and closing one HTTP port at a time."""
import json,os,subprocess,sys,time
from pathlib import Path
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
env={**os.environ,'PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE':'1','PYTHONUTF8':'1','PYTHONIOENCODING':'utf-8',
     'TMP':str(OUT/'tmp'),'TEMP':str(OUT/'tmp'),'TMPDIR':str(OUT/'tmp')}
names=(['run_verification.py'] if 'tests' in sys.argv else ['perf_probe.py'] if 'perf' in sys.argv
       else ['run_http.py','supplemental_http.py','attack_http.py','m5_http.py','cross_item_http.py','perf_check_http.py'])
rows=[]
for name in names:
    start=time.perf_counter()
    p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/name)],cwd=ROOT,env=env,capture_output=True,timeout=240)
    (OUT/(name+'.stdout.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
    rows.append({'script':name,'exit_code':p.returncode,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-start})
    (OUT/('execution_'+sys.argv[-1]+'.json')).write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
    print(json.dumps(rows[-1]),flush=True)
    if p.returncode: raise SystemExit(p.returncode)

### independent_m14_http.py
"""Independent CLI HTTP confirmation of candidate round-nine findings."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
PORT = 8944
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
CASES = ['3Hvinblastine', '3HVinblastine', '3HVINBLASTINE', '3hvinblastine', '[3h]-vinblastine', '3H-vinblastine', 'vinblastine', '3Hvinblastine, osimertinib', '89ZrpanitumumabPETCT', '89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT', '89Zr-panitumumab-PET-CT', '89zr-panitumumab-pet/ct', '[89Zr] panitumumab', 'panitumumab', '89ZrDFO-daratumumab', 'A33124I', 'A33 124i', 'ADAKITUG', 'rad001c', '13-cis retinoic acid', 'osimertinib 80 mg', 'paclitaxel 2 cycles', 'cisplatin 75 mg/m2 day 1', 'BKM-120NX']


def post(endpoint, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + "/api/" + endpoint,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    with response:
        return {"endpoint": endpoint, "input": text, "status": response.status,
                "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start,
                "response": json.loads(response.read())}

with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "owned port already occupied"
db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"}
with (OUT / "independent_m14_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    results = []
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        for name in CASES:
            for endpoint in ("resolve", "explore"):
                results.append(post(endpoint, name))
            if not name.startswith("CHEMBL"):
                results.append(post("ask", f"Which trials evaluate {name}?"))
                results.append(post("ask", f"Who sponsors trials of {name}?"))
        (OUT / "independent_m14_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(results,
            ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps([{"input": r["input"], "status": r["status"],
            "center": r["response"].get("center", {}).get("id"),
            "refused": r["response"].get("refused")}
            for r in results if r["endpoint"] == "explore"], indent=2))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        (OUT / "independent_m14_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "port_closed": closed,
            "database_unchanged": hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == before,
            "requests": len(results), "all_200": all(r["status"] == 200 for r in results),
            "page_matches_worktree": page == (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_bytes()
        }, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

# them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
# nanoparticles").
_JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
            "or/and", "and/or"}



# every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
_DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
                        if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"


def _plain(text: str) -> str:
    """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
    return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))


def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
    """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
    return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))


def _fold(text: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))



# Mass numbers of the isotopes used in medicine, per element (lower case).
_MEDICAL_MASS = {"h": {2, 3}, "c": {11, 13, 14}, "n": {13, 15}, "o": {15}, "f": {18},
                 "p": {32}, "s": {35}, "cu": {61, 62, 64, 67}, "ga": {67, 68}, "br": {76, 77},
                 "rb": {82}, "sr": {89, 90}, "y": {86, 90}, "zr": {89}, "tc": {99}, "in": {111},
                 "i": {123, 124, 125, 131}, "sm": {153}, "lu": {177}, "re": {186, 188},
                 "pb": {203, 212}, "at": {211}, "ra": {223}, "ac": {225}}


def _medical_isotope(label: str) -> bool:
    m = re.match(r"^(\d{1,3})m?([a-z]{1,2})$", (label or "").lower())
    return bool(m and int(m.group(1)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(2), set()))

def _isotope_edge(word: str) -> bool:
    """An isotope label fused to the start or the end of one word ("89ZrDFO", "DFO89Zr").

    A one-letter element counts only where the case marks the boundary ("89FDFO"), so
    dose text such as "2cycles" or "5in" is not read as carbon or indium."""
    for i in range(2, min(6, len(word)) + 1):
        for lab, rest in ((word[:i], word[i:]), (word[-i:], word[:-i])):
            # the label must meet letters, not run on into more digits ("89Zr" is whole)
            if not rest or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
                continue
            if not (rest[:1] if lab == word[:i] else rest[-1:]).isalpha():
                continue
            elem = re.sub(r"^[\d\s\-\[\]m]*", "", lab)
            if len(elem) == 2 and elem[0].isupper() and elem[1].islower():
                return True                      # "Zr", "Ga", "Lu"
            if len(elem) == 1 and elem.isupper() and (rest[:1].isupper() if lab == word[:i]
                                                      else rest[-1:].isupper()):
                return True                      # "89FDFO"-like, case-marked
    return False

def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
    """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?

    The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
    biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
    work grows linearly with the item."""
    def hit(text: str) -> bool:
        # the alias key ignores punctuation, underscores and accents, so the guards also
        # see the text with those folded ("bevacizumab-(awwb)", "bevacizumab_awwb",
        # "bevácizumab-awwb")
        plain = _plain(text)
        # and with every separator (punctuation, underscore, spaces) read as one space,
        # so "generic-version of", "albumin - bound" meet the multiword patterns
        return any(T.is_distinct_product(v) or T.is_radiotracer(v)
                   for v in {text, plain, re.sub(r"(?:[^\w\s]|_)+", "-", plain).strip("-"),
                             " ".join(_alnum_words(plain))})

    if hit(" ".join(words)):
        return True
    if any(T.canonical_key(w) in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES for w in words):
        return True
    return any(hit(" ".join(words[i:j])) for i in range(len(words))
               for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1))

class OncoKG:
    def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
                 resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
        self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
        self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
        self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
        self._resolve_cache: dict = {}
        self.disease = resolved["disease"]
        self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
                        if t.get("symbol")}
        # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
        # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
        self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
        for row in self.conn.execute(
                "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
            self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
            self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
        # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
        for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
            if same in self.companies:
                self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
        self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}

    # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
    def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
        # one spelling for every check: compatibility forms folded, every dash a hyphen
        question = _fold(question)
        blocked = self._resolver_blocked(question)
        found = self._link(question)
        whole = self._whole_name(question, blocked, found)
        if whole:
            return [whole]
        if not blocked:
            return found
        # a drug the resolver refuses in this text is refused here too, whatever the
        # splitting below made of it (the two must agree before anything is linked)
        out = [e for e in found if not (e["type"] == "Drug" and e["id"] in blocked)]
        for text in sorted({t for t in blocked.values()}):
            if not any(e["type"] == "Refused" and (text in e["label"] or e["label"] in text)
                       for e in out):
                out.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text, "matched_text": text,
                            "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not the "
                                   "originator molecule"})
        return out

    def _resolver_blocked(self, text: str) -> dict:
        """Drug ids inside the pieces the intervention resolver refuses as a distinct
        product or a tracer, with the refused piece."""
        comps = []
        lone = set()
        pieces = {p.strip() for p in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001/|]", text) if p.strip()}
        for piece in pieces:                         # each distinct list piece once
            words = [w for w in re.split(r"[^\w\-]+", piece) if w]
            if not self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
                continue                             # no drug in it: nothing to refuse
            got = self._resolve_cache.get(piece)
            if got is None:
                try:
                    got = N.resolve_drug_string(piece, self.dic, self.index_keys)["components"]
                except Exception:                    # never let a guard crash a lookup
                    got = []
                if len(self._resolve_cache) >= 4096:
                    self._resolve_cache.clear()
                self._resolve_cache[piece] = got
            if len(words) == 1 and T.canonical_key(piece) in self.dic["index"]:
                lone.add(piece)                      # one stored name on its own ("566C")
            comps += got
        out: dict = {}
        accepted = {c.get("drug_id") for c in comps
                    if c.get("drug_id") and not str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED")}
        for c in comps:
            piece = c.get("component") or ""
            tracer = "radiotracer_guard" in (c.get("rules") or []) or T.is_radiotracer(piece)
            if not (c.get("tier") == "BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT"
                    or (str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED") and tracer)):
                continue
            if piece in lone:
                continue
            words = [w for w in re.split(r"[\s/|,;]+", piece) if w]
            words = [w.strip(" ?.!:()[]") or w for w in words]
            for a, b in self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
                key = T.canonical_key(" ".join(words[a:b]))
                for h in self.dic["index"].get(key, []):
                    # the same molecule named separately and accepted ("biosimilar
                    # anastrozole, Arimidex") is left to the list-item rule below
                    if h["drug_id"] not in accepted:
                        out[h["drug_id"]] = piece
        return out

    def _keys_of(self, did: str) -> set:
        """Every lookup key that names this drug."""
        if not hasattr(self, "_drug_keys"):
            self._drug_keys: dict = {}
            for k, hits in self.dic["index"].items():
                for h in hits:
                    self._drug_keys.setdefault(h["drug_id"], set()).add(k)
        return self._drug_keys.get(did, set())

    def _marked(self, key: str, did: str, tokens, seg, a: int, b: int):
        """The text that makes this drug name a distinct product or a tracer, if any.

        Checked on the same letters-and-digits form the name was looked up by: a known
        biosimilar suffix at the end of the name ("bevacizumab awwb", "bevacizumab_awwb")
        or as the next word, or an isotope label just before or after it ("[89.Zr]
        trastuzumab", "89:Zr trastuzumab")."""
        for sfx in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES:
            stem = key[:-len(sfx)]
            if key.endswith(sfx) and stem and any(
                    h["drug_id"] == did for h in self.dic["index"].get(stem, [])):
                return " ".join(tokens[a:b])
        same = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == seg[a]]
        before = [k for k in same if k < a]
        after = [k for k in same if k >= b]
        # words counted after normalisation, so punctuation never uses up the budget
        prev = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-2:]
        nxt = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:2]
        own = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))
        lone = not before and not after      # the name is the whole list item
        # an isotope label anywhere in the name ("A33 124i"), or just before or after it
        # ("[89.Zr] trastuzumab", "trastuzumab 89 - Zr"); an isotope-shaped name counts
        # as a name only when it stands alone ("566C", "18f"), never as the substance
        # of a longer phrase ("18F fluorothymidine")
        inside = [own[i:j] for i in range(len(own)) for j in (i + 1, i + 2) if j <= len(own)]
        # and every run of the name's words glued as the key glues them, so a symbol
        # split by punctuation inside a stored alias is still an isotope ("[89Z.r]-DFO-")
        for i in range(len(own)):
            for j in range(i + 1, len(own) + 1):
                glued = "".join(own[i:j])
                if len(glued) > 6:
                    break
                if j - i > 1:
                    inside.append([glued])
        if lone and T.is_isotope_label("".join(own)):
            inside = []                      # the whole item is one isotope-shaped name
        name = " ".join(tokens[a:b])
        # the neighbours glued back together the way the lookup key glues them, so a
        # suffix or an isotope spelt in pieces is still seen ("a - w w b", "[89Z.r]")
        nxt_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:8]
        prev_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-8:]
        for n in range(1, len(nxt_all) + 1):
            glued = "".join(nxt_all[:n]).lower()
            if glued in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES or T.is_isotope_label(glued):
                return f"{name} ({' '.join(nxt_all[:n])})"
            if len(glued) > 6:
                break
        for n in range(1, len(prev_all) + 1):
            glued = "".join(prev_all[-n:]).lower()
            if T.is_isotope_label(glued):
                return f"({' '.join(prev_all[-n:])}) {name}"
            if len(glued) > 6:
                break
        # a label fused to a neighbouring word, such as a chelator ("DFO89Zr", "89ZrDFO")
        for w in nxt_all[:2] + prev_all[-2:]:
            if _isotope_edge(w):
                return f"{name} ({w})"
        for ws in inside + [prev[-1:], prev, nxt[:1], nxt]:
            if ws and T.is_isotope_label(" ".join(ws)):
                return name if ws in inside else f"{name} ({' '.join(ws)})"
        # an isotope glued to the name itself ("89zr-trastuzumab" read through "[89Z.r]")
        # anything may sit between them (a chelator: "89ZrDFO-daratumumab"), so the rest
        # needs only to end, or start, with a name of the same drug
        # A one-letter element is left to the case-marked word check above: lower-case
        # chemistry ("13-cis retinoic acid") is not carbon-13.
        # The drug's name may sit anywhere in the rest ("89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT"). A
        # one-letter element counts where it is written upper-case in the name
        # ("3HVINBLASTINE", "A33124I"); lower-case chemistry ("13-cis") does not.
        names = [k for k in self._keys_of(did) if len(k) >= 5]
        orig = "".join(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))))
        if len(orig) != len(key):
            orig = key.upper()                   # cannot align: treat as marked
        for i in range(2, min(6, len(key))):
            for lab, tail, at in ((key[:i], key[i:], i - 1), (key[-i:], key[:-i], len(key) - 1)):
                if not tail or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
                    continue
                elem = re.sub(r"^\d+m?", "", lab)
                if len(elem) == 1 and not orig[at].isupper():
                    continue
                if any(k in tail for k in names):
                    return name
                # a label closing a stored name of this drug ("A33124I"): the name itself
                # is the labelled product. Only a mass used in medicine counts here, so a
                # development code's salt letter ("NSC-118218H", "DX-8951F") is not a label
                if (lab == key[-i:] and len(tail) >= 2 and key in self._keys_of(did)
                        and _medical_isotope(lab)):
                    return name

        return None

    def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
        """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
        "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.

        Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list
        linker refused, and no product or tracer pattern in the name."""
        if blocked or any(e["type"] == "Refused" for e in found):
            return None
        name = text.strip(" ?!.")
        if T.is_distinct_product(name) or T.is_radiotracer(name):
            return None             # the list linker above has already applied the rest
        key = T.canonical_key(name)
        exact = [h for h in (self.dic["index"].get(key) or [])
                 if _fold(h["alias"]).strip(" ?!.").lower() == name.lower()]
        if not exact or len({h["drug_id"] for h in exact}) != 1:
            return None
        did = exact[0]["drug_id"]
        return {"type": "Drug", "id": did, "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
                "matched_text": name, "matched_alias": exact[0]["alias"]}

    def _link(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
        found: list[dict] = []
        seen: set = set()
        tokens: list[str] = []
        clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
        inner: list[bool] = []          # token inside parentheses
        ci, depth = 0, 0
        for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):
            for raw in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
                # a slash or bar, glued or free, ends the piece ("bevacizumab - awwb/
                # osimertinib"); it must not hide a product suffix from the guards
                for j, part in enumerate(re.split(r"[/|]", raw)):
                    if j:
                        ci += 1
                    if not part:
                        continue
                    if re.search(r"\w", part):
                        # edge punctuation and wrappers off ("bevacizumab-awwb?",
                        # "(bevacizumab-awwb)"); an isotope ("[89Zr]") is checked as a word
                        t = re.sub(r"^[^\w]+|[^\w]+$", "", part)
                    elif part in ("-", "\u2013", "\u2014", "+", "&"):
                        t = part            # "bevacizumab - awwb" needs its dash
                    else:
                        t = ""
                    if t:
                        tokens.append(t)
                        clause.append(ci)
                        inner.append(depth > 0 or part.startswith("("))
                    depth = max(0, depth + part.count("(") - part.count(")"))
            ci += 1

        # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
        # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
        # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
        # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
        # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
        # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
        # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
        # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
        seg: list[int] = []
        n = 0
        for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
            # "and" inside parentheses describes one product ("doxorubicin
            # (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)"); it does not list two
            if t.lower() in _JOINERS and not inner[k]:
                n += 1
                seg.append(-1)
                continue
            if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
                n += 1
            seg.append(n)
        refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
                             if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
        drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0}, seg)
        dangling = None                 # a qualifier item with no drug, joined by "and"/"or"
        prev_end = -1
        for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
            idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
            text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
            joined = (dangling is not None and idx[0] > prev_end + 1
                      and all(seg[k] < 0 for k in range(prev_end + 1, idx[0]))
                      and clause[idx[0]] == clause[prev_end])
            prev_end = idx[-1]
            hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
            if _qualified([tokens[k] for k in idx]):
                named = any(k in refused and (
                    T.canonical_key(tokens[k]) in self.dic["index"]
                    or tokens[k].lower() in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES)
                    for k in idx)
                if not hit and not named:
                    # "liposomal an

+"""Hash tracked bytes and adapt existing reproducers into round 15 only."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import hashlib, json, subprocess
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+tracked = subprocess.check_output(['git', 'ls-files', '-z'], cwd=ROOT).decode('utf8').split('\0')
+initial = dict(
+    head=subprocess.check_output(['git', 'rev-parse', 'HEAD'], cwd=ROOT).decode().strip(),
+    tracked_sha256={p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT/p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in tracked if p},
+    status=subprocess.check_output(['git', 'status', '--porcelain'], cwd=ROOT).decode(),
+    db_sha256=hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
+)
+assert not (OUT/'integrity_before.json').exists(), 'Do not overwrite initial snapshot'
+(OUT/'integrity_before.json').write_text(json.dumps(initial, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
+manifest = []
+for sub in ['root_checks', 'cost']:
+    dest = OUT/sub
+    (dest/'tmp').mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+    for src in sorted((ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m14'/sub).glob('*.py')):
+        code = src.read_text(encoding='utf8')
+        changes = []
+        for old, new in [('audit_evidence_claims/m14/', 'audit_evidence_claims/m15/')]:
+            if old in code:
+                code = code.replace(old, new)
+                changes.append(f'{old} -> {new}')
+        (dest/src.name).write_text(code, encoding='utf8')
+        manifest.append(dict(source=src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(), copy=(dest/src.name).relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(), sha256=hashlib.sha256(src.read_bytes()).hexdigest(), changes=changes, output_root='__file__ relative m15; exact historical case inputs retained'))
+src = ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m14/finalize_integrity.py'
+(OUT/'finalize_integrity.py').write_text(src.read_text(encoding='utf8').replace('8952]', '8952,8945]'), encoding='utf8')
+(OUT/'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
+print(json.dumps(dict(head=initial['head'], tracked=len(initial['tracked_sha256']), scripts=len(manifest))))

+# Mass numbers of the isotopes used in medicine, per element (lower case).
+_MEDICAL_MASS = {"h": {2, 3}, "c": {11, 13, 14}, "n": {13, 15}, "o": {15}, "f": {18},
+                 "p": {32}, "s": {35}, "cu": {61, 62, 64, 67}, "ga": {67, 68}, "br": {76, 77},

+                 "pb": {203, 212}, "at": {211}, "ra": {223}, "ac": {225}}

+
+def _medical_isotope(label: str) -> bool:
+    m = re.match(r"^(\d{1,3})m?([a-z]{1,2})$", (label or "").lower())
+    return bool(m and int(m.group(1)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(2), set()))

"""Run the unmodified existing suite with scratch confined to this directory."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys
import time

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
TMP = OUT / "tmp"
TMP.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP": str(TMP), "TEMP": str(TMP),
       "TMPDIR": str(TMP)}

def git(*args):
    result = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
    return {"code": result.returncode, "stdout": result.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
            "stderr": result.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}

files = ["src/app.py", "src/query.py", "web/try.html", "tests/test_app.py",
         "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"]
before = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
(OUT / "verification_initial.json").write_text(json.dumps({"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"),
    "status": git("status", "--short"), "hashes": before}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "tests", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"]
start = time.perf_counter()
run = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True)
(OUT / "pytest.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
after = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
summary = {"command": "python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider",
           "environment": {k: env[k] for k in ("PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE", "PYTHONUTF8", "PYTHONIOENCODING", "TMP", "TEMP", "TMPDIR")},
           "exit_code": run.returncode, "wall_seconds": round(time.perf_counter() - start, 3),
           "hashes_unchanged": before == after, "hashes_after": after}
(OUT / "pytest_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print((run.stdout + run.stderr).decode("utf-8", "replace"))
print(json.dumps(summary, indent=2))
raise SystemExit(run.returncode)
"""Run exact M8/M9 HTTP reproductions with scratch and environment confined to M10."""
import json, os, subprocess, sys, time
from pathlib import Path
OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
env = dict(os.environ, PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1', PYTHONUTF8='1', PYTHONIOENCODING='utf-8',
           TMP=str(OUT/'tmp'), TEMP=str(OUT/'tmp'), TMPDIR=str(OUT/'tmp'))
rows = []
for name in ['new_http.py', 'independent_confirm.py']:
    start = time.perf_counter()
    p = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-B', str(OUT/name)], cwd=ROOT, env=env,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=180)
    (OUT/(name+'.stdout.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
    rows.append(dict(script=name, exit_code=p.returncode, seconds=time.perf_counter()-start))
    (OUT/'execution_extra.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
    print(json.dumps(rows[-1]), flush=True)
    if p.returncode: raise SystemExit(p.returncode)

FFIX = re.compile(
    r"^(?P<inn>[A-Za-z]{6,}(?:mab|cept|ase|grastim|stim|poetin))\s*-\s*(?P<sfx>[A-Za-z]{4})$")


def strip_fda_suffix(text: str) -> str | None:
    m = RE_FDA_SUFFIX.match((text or "").strip())
    if not m:
        return None
    sfx = m.group("sfx")
    if sfx.lower() in SUFFIX_LOOKALIKES or not (sfx.islower() or sfx.isupper()):
        return None
    return m.group("inn")


# "No pembrolizumab", "without bevacizumab": a negated substance is the
# absence of an exposure. Accepting it as CONTAINED_ALIAS recorded the
# opposite of what the arm says.
RE_NEGATED = re.compile(
    r"^\s*(?:no|not|without|none\s+of|absence\s+of|omit(?:ting|ted)?|"
    r"withdrawal\s+of|discontinu(?:e|ed|ation\s+of))\b[\s\-]*", re.I)


# "pembrolizumab-free regimen", "free of steroids": an absence written after
# the substance rather than before it
# ("free base" is a chemical form, not an absence)
RE_NEGATED_AFTER = re.compile(
    r"\b[A-Za-z0-9]+[\s\-]free\b(?![\s\-]*base)|\bfree\s+of\b", re.I)


def is_negated(text: str) -> bool:
    t = text or ""
    return bool(RE_NEGATED.match(t) or RE_NEGATED_AFTER.search(t))

# A placebo arm must never create a "this company administered this drug" edge.
RE_PLACEBO = re.compile(r"\bplacebo\b|\bsham\b|\bvehicle\s+control\b", re.I)

# Combination separators, longest first so "combined with" wins over "with".
RE_COMBO = re.compile(
    r"\s*(?:\+|&|,|、|，|/(?=\s*[A-Za-z])|\bcombined\s+with\b|"
    r"\bin\s+combination\s+with\b|\bcombination\s+with\b|\bplus\b|\band\b|\bor\b|"
    r"\bfollowed\s+by\b)\s*",
    re.I,
)

RE_PAREN = re.compile(r"\(([^()]*)\)|\[([^\[\]]*)\]")

# Development codes ("AZD9291", "BMS-986504", "PF-07224826", "TSC-204-A0201").
# One character apart is a different molecule, so these must never be matched
# approximately -- the usual string-similarity intuition is wrong here.
RE_DEV_CODE = re.compile(
    r"^[A-Za-z]{1,5}[\s\-]?\d{2,8}(?:[\s\-][A-Za-z0-9]{1,8})*$")

# Trailing lot/format suffixes that do not change the substance: "BMS-936558-01"
RE_DEV_CODE_SUFFIX = re.compile(r"^(?P<core>[A-Za-z]{1,5}[\s\-]?\d{3,8})[\s\-]0?\d{1,2}$")
RE_MULTISPACE = re.compile(r"\s+")
RE_PUNCT_EDGE = re.compile(r"^[\s\-–—,;:.、，。]+|[\s\-–—,;:.、，。]+$")


# Isotope labels on radiotracers: 18F, 68Ga, 99mTc, 177Lu, 64Cu, 89Zr, 131I.
# These look exactly like development codes but name an isotope, not a molecule,
# and the substance they label is usually not in a therapeutic vocabulary at all.
RE_ISOTOPE = re.compile(r"^\[?(\d{1,3}m?)[\s\-]?([A-Za-z]{1,2})\]?$")
ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS = {
    "f", "c", "n", "o", "ga", "cu", "zr", "i", "lu", "tc", "in", "y", "at",
    "ac", "pb", "re", "sm", "sr", "ra", "rb", "br", "h", "p", "s",
}


def is_isotope_label(text: str) -> bool:
    m = RE_ISOTOPE.match((text or "").strip())
    return bool(m and m.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)


# "[18F]F-AraG", "68Ga-DOTATATE": an isotope prefix marks the whole string as a
# radiolabelled tracer, whose unlabelled moiety is not the administered drug.
RE_RADIOTRACER = re.compile(
    r"(?:^|[\s\(\[])\[?\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]?\s*[-–]\s*\w"
    r"|(?:^|[\s\(])\[\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]\s*[A-Za-z]", re.I)


def is_radiotracer(text: str) -> bool:
    t = (text or "").strip()
    m = RE_RADIOTRACER.search(t)
    if not m:
        return False
    head = re.match(r"^[\s\(\[]*\[?(\d{1,3})m?[\s\-]?([A-Za-z]{1,2})\]?", t)
    return bool(head and head.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)


def is_dev_code(text: str) -> bool:
    """True for alphanumeric development codes, where fuzzy matching is unsafe."""
    t = (text or "").strip()
    return bool(RE_DEV_CODE.match(t))


def dev_code_core(text: str) -> str | None:
    """Strip a trailing lot/format number: 'BMS-936558-01' -> 'BMS-936558'."""
    m = RE_D
"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
click to move on),
then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
        """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.

        `text` is a node id or a name the question linker reads as one entity. A name
        the linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is reported, never
        mapped to the originator. Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the
        page states: trials by start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph,
        sponsors by trials they lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship
        keeps its role (lead sponsor or collaborator)."""
        conn = self.kg.conn
        also: list = []
        matched = None
        row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                           (text,)).fetchone()
        if row:
            nid = row["id"]
        else:
            ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
            refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
            hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
            if not hits:
                if refused:
                    return {"refused": refused[

import json
import posixpath
import re

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent

def read(rel):
    return (OUT / rel).read_text(encoding="utf8")

def data(rel):
    return json.loads(read(rel))

def links(text, prefix):
    return re.sub(r"\]\(([^)]+)\)", lambda m: "](" + posixpath.normpath(prefix + "/" + m.group(1)) + ")", text)

100: # every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
101: _DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
102:                         if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"

105: def _plain(text: str) -> str:
106:     """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
107:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
108:     return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))

111: def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
112:     """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
113:     return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))

117:     return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))

129: def _medical_isotope(label: str) -> bool:
130:     m = re.match(r"^(\d{1,3})m?([a-z]{1,2})$", (label or "").lower())
131:     return bool(m and int(m.group(1)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(2), set()))

133: def _isotope_edge(word: str) -> bool:
134:     """An isotope label fused to the start or the end of one word ("89ZrDFO", "DFO89Zr").
135: 
136:     A one-letter element counts only where the case marks the boundary ("89FDFO"), so
137:     dose text such as "2cycles" or "5in" is not read as carbon or indium."""
138:     for i in range(2, min(6, len(word)) + 1):
139:         for lab, rest in ((word[:i], word[i:]), (word[-i:], word[:-i])):
140:             # the label must meet letters, not run on into more digits ("89Zr" is whole)
141:             if not rest or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
142:                 continue
143:             if not (rest[:1] if lab == word[:i] else rest[-1:]).isalpha():
144:                 continue
145:             elem = re.sub(r"^[\d\s\-\[\]m]*", "", lab)
146:             if len(elem) == 2 and elem[0].isupper() and elem[1].islower():
147:                 return True                      # "Zr", "Ga", "Lu"
148:             if len(elem) == 1 and elem.isupper() and (rest[:1].isupper() if lab == word[:i]
149:                                                       else rest[-1:].isupper()):
150:                 return True                      # "89FDFO"-like, case-marked
151:     return False
152:

154:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
155: 
156:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
157:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
158:     work grows linearly with the item."""
296:         lone = not before and not after      # the name is the whole list item

351:                 if not tail or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):

353:                 elem = re.sub(r"^\d+m?", "", lab)
354:                 if len(elem) == 1 and not orig[at].isupper():
355:                     continue
356:                 if any(k in tail for k in names):
357:                     return name
358:                 # a label closing a stored name of this drug ("A33124I"): the name itself
359:                 # is the labelled product. Only a mass used in medicine counts here, so a
360:                 # development code's salt letter ("NSC-118218H", "DX-8951F") is not a label
361:                 if (lab == key[-i:] and len(tail) >= 2 and key in self._keys_of(did)
362:                         and _medical_isotope(lab)):
363:                     return name
364: 
365:         return None
366: 
367:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
368:         """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
369:         "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.
370: 
371:         Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list

# Independent round-15 tracer adjudication

Reviewed HEAD `1b3896669387cbc142c12b726909231cca8658db`. This assessment reuses the prior independent staged adjudicator, changing its candidate set; the current and HEAD^ query modules run against the same current app, rules and database. ## P1: punctuation inside an adjacent chelator-label word defeats every refusal stage

`89-ZrDFO trastuzumab`, `89Z.rDFO trastuzumab`, and `trastuzumab DFO89-Zr` all draw **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, with 13 live trials, no refused entity, and **12/12** qualified trial/sponsor QA evidence rows. Appending `, osimertinib` still selects TRASTUZUMAB. Both current and HEAD^ have the leak.

Controls `89ZrDFO trastuzumab` and `trastuzumab DFO89Zr` refuse and yield **0/0** QA rows. The corresponding canonical keys are unchanged by the added punctuation. Thus this differs from the earlier descriptive-distance cases: a single hyphen is inserted inside an immediately adjacent, recognized label/chelator word, without adding a descriptive word, changing the drug, or moving the label farther away. `89Zr labelled trastuzumab` and `trastuzumab labelled with 89Zr` were also rerun and remain inherited supplementary failures, not new discoveries.

| Stage | All three new candidate spellings |
|---|---|
| Raw intervention resolver | CONTAINED_ALIAS, CHEMBL1201585; leftover chelator text UNRESOLVED |
| Raw tracer predicate / `_qualified` | false / false |
| Resolver veto | `{}` |
| `_marked` | `None` |
| Item/public linker | Drug CHEMBL1201585; no Refused |
| Whole-name shortcut | `None` |
| Map | TRASTUZUMAB, 13 live trials |
| Qualified trial/sponsor QA | 12 / 12 evidence rows |

The causal trace is especially direct for the hyphen cases: `_isotope_edge('89-ZrDFO')` and `_isotope_edge('DFO89-Zr')` already return **true**. `_marked` does not pass those original words to the helper. Instead `_alnum_words` splits them (`src/query.py:111-113,316-317`) into `['89','ZrDFO']` or `['DFO89','Zr']`. The glued-neighbour loops test only whether the entire glued string is an isotope, which it is not because it also includes DFO (`src/query.py:318-329`). The subsequent fused-label helper receives only individual fragments (`src/query.py:330-333`), all of which return false. The raw tracer regex also misses these forms (`src/textrules.py:303-314`), so the resolver cannot provide a veto (`src/normalize.py:738-746`; `src/query.py:249-265`). The final own-name key scan examines only `trastuzumab`, not the neighboring word (`src/query.py:345-363`). The accepted hit becomes the map centre (`src/app.py:88-95`).

Required outcome: preserve the refusal of a recognized adjacent isotope/chelator label under the same punctuation normalization that lookup uses. No application repair was made in this audit.

## Exact historical residuals: case-sensitive stored tracer keys

| Exact input | Current | HEAD^ | Classification |
|---|---|---|---|
| `3HVINBLASTINE`, `3Hvinblastine` | refused, 0/0 QA | VINBLASTINE, 5/5 QA | **FIXED** exact uppercase cases |
| `3hvinblastine` | VINBLASTINE / CHEMBL159, 5/5 QA | same | **NOT FIXED**, historical M14 variant |
| `[3h]-vinblastine`, `3H-vinblastine` | refused, 0/0 QA | same | Refused controls |
| `A33124I` | refused, 0/0 QA | ADAKITUG | **FIXED** exact M13/M14 uppercase residual |
| `A33124i` | ADAKITUG / CHEMBL5314551, 0/0 QA | same | **NOT FIXED**, lowercase closing-label variant |
| `A33 124i` | refused, 0/0 QA | same | Refused control |

The dictionary itself links canonical `3hvinblastine` to `[3h]-vinblastine`, and `a33124i` to `A33 124i`; controls establish refused identity without external pharmacology assumptions. For the two lowercase inputs every refusal stage misses, and the case condition explicitly skips the one-letter label (`src/query.py:353-355`). `_medical_isotope('124i')` is reachable only after that case condition, so the medical-mass exception does not help the lowercase closing label.

The user explicitly described the repair as uppercase-sensitive. These results document that repair's exact boundary rather than mislabeling uppercase fixes as failed. The original no-originator-leak approval guarantee still has these residuals, and the user did not list these tracer under-refusals among accepted over-refusals or the separate ChEMBL biosimilar-code limitation. They should not be counted as new independent families merely to inflate the finding count. The new neighboring-chelator punctuation finding is sufficient for CHANGES REQUIRED by itself.

## M15-B1 — splitting an isotope in a neighbouring chelator token defeats the check that recognizes its raw form

**Exact request:** `POST /api/explore`, JSON `{"text":"89-ZrDFO trastuzumab"}`.

**Observed:** HTTP 200, **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, **13 live trials**, no refusal. `Which trials evaluate 89-ZrDFO trastuzumab?` and `Who sponsors trials of 89-ZrDFO trastuzumab?` each return **12 originator evidence rows** (the QA result cap). The reverse **`trastuzumab DFO89-Zr`** does the same. The split-symbol alternatives **`89Z.rDFO trastuzumab`** and **`trastuzumab DFO89Z.r`** also leak. Adding `, osimertinib` still selects TRASTUZUMAB for the split-symbol input. [Full stages](targeted_results.json), [HTTP](targeted_http.json), [compact matrix](targeted_summary.json).

**Controls:** `89ZrDFO trastuzumab` and `trastuzumab DFO89Zr` refuse and have zero qualified trial/sponsor evidence. Each failing/control pair has the same canonical key; adding a hyphen inside `89Zr` is sufficient to change refusal into originator selection. No full DFO/trastuzumab key is present in the dictionary, so this is a neighbour-check failure rather than a consumed-whole-alias failure. Bare `trastuzumab` remains available. [Dictionary and helper evidence](dictionary_helper_evidence.json).

**Precise cause:** `_isotope_edge('89-ZrDFO')` and `_isotope_edge('DFO89-Zr')` both return **True** (`src/query.py:138-147`). However, `_marked` first uses `_alnum_words` to turn the preceding/following token into `['89', 'ZrDFO']` or `['DFO89', 'Zr']` (`src/query.py:293-294,316-317`). Its fused-label helper is then called on each fragment, which is false for all of them (`src/query.py:330-333`). Its separate gluing loops check only *whole* isotope labels, so `89zrDFO`/`DFO89zr` is not accepted by those checks (`src/query.py:318-329`). The later name-key fallback sees only `trastuzumab`, not the adjacent marker (`src/query.py:345-363`). `_marked` returns `None` in the captured actual `_link` call. This is a precise normalization inconsistency: the raw token is already recognizable by the existing helper, but that recognizable form is discarded before it is used.

Neither the raw resolver's guard (`src/normalize.py:738-746`; `src/textrules.py:303-314`) nor `_qualified` recognizes these forms. `Engine.explore` then draws the surviving first positive hit (`src/app.py:88-95`). Both current and HEAD^ query modules leak against the same current app/data: **inherited**, not introduced by the latest patch. This comparison changes only the query module in memory, not an entire historical dep
), while **`A33 124i`** refuses and repaired **`A33124I`** now refuses. Both share the stored key `a33124i`. `_medical_isotope('124i')` is true, but the lower-case check skips the iteration before the medical-mass closing-label branch (`src/query.py:354-362`). The original upper-case M13/M14 reproduction is **FIXED**; the lower-case equivalent remains an originator leak. This is corroboration of the remaining case-sensitive stored-alias gap, not a separately inflated new finding.

## Specification-axis summary

The changed upper-case, trailing-marker and upper-case medical-mass reproductions work. The original no-originator-leak guarantee still fails in two ways: a new reproduced punctuation boundary in the neighbouring chelator check, and the explicitly recorded lower-case member of M14-B1. +    head=subprocess.check_output(['git', 'rev-parse', 'HEAD'], cwd=ROOT).decode().strip(),

+    status=subprocess.check_output(['git', 'status', '--porcelain'], cwd=ROOT).decode(),

+)

+        code = src.read_text(encoding='utf8')
+        changes = []
+        for old, new in [('audit_evidence_claims/m14/', 'audit_evidence_claims/m15/')]:
+            if old in code:
+                code = code.replace(old, new)
+                changes.append(f'{old} -> {new}')
+        (dest/src.name).write_text(code, encoding='utf8')
+        manifest.append(dict(source=src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(), copy=(dest/src.name).relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(), sha256=hashlib.sha256(src.read_bytes()).hexdigest(), changes=changes, output_root='__file__ relative m15; exact historical case inputs retained'))

+"""Assemble the independent round-15 report from this round's fresh evidence."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import json, posixpath, re
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+def read(rel):
+    return (OUT/rel).read_text(encoding='utf8')
+def data(rel):
+    return json.loads(read(rel))
+def links(text, prefix):
+    return re.sub(r'\]\(([^)]+)\)', lambda m: ']('+posixpath.normpath(prefix+'/'+m.group(1))+')', text)
+
+head = data('integrity_before.json')['head']
+name_rows = data('names/historical_name_dispositions.json')
+assert len(name_rows) == 69
+assert data('db_ui/db_validate.json')['errors'] == []
+assert data('db_ui/db_assertions.json')['passed'] == 19
+assert data('db_ui/ui_assertions.json')['passed'] == 24
+assert data('root_checks/pytest_summary.json')['exit_code'] == 0
+assert len(data('cost/execution_isolated.json')) == 9
+assert all(r['exit_code'] == 0 for r in data('cost/execution_isolated.json'))
+
+attack = read('attacks/FINDINGS.md')
+blockers = '## M15-B1'+attack.split('## M15-B1',1)[1].split('## Replays, controls and exploratory limits',1)[0]
+blockers = blockers.replace('## Residual M14-B1 —', '## M15-B2 — residual M14-B1:')
+blockers = links(blockers, 'attacks')
+names = links(read('names/FINDINGS.md').split('## Individual historical dispositions',1)[1].split('## Repair interpretation and limits',1)[0], 'names')
+db = links(read('db_ui/FINDINGS.md').split('## Historical findings: individual dispositions',1)[1].split('## Standards axis',1)[0], 'db_ui')
+norm = {(r['ref'],r['name']):r for r in data('cost/normalization_cost.json')}
+assert len(norm) == 24 and all('median_seconds' in r for r in norm.values())
+def median(ref, name):
+    return f"{norm[ref,name]['median_seconds']:.5f}"
+unique = data('cost/isolated_unique_cost_http.json')
+unicode = data('cost/isolated_unicode_cost_http.json')
+split = {r['ref']:r['median'] for r in data('cost/fresh_cost_comparison.json') if r['kind']=='split' and r['size']==2000}
+repeat = data('cost/fresh_cost_http.json')
+plain = data('cost/perf_check_http.json')
+http = data('root_checks/http_results.json')
+perf = next(r for r in data('cost/perf_current.json') if r['kind']=='plain' and r['size']==250)
+
+intro = f'''# Independent relation-map review — round 15

+"""Hash tracked bytes and adapt existing reproducers into round 15 only."""
+from pathlib import Path
+import hashlib, json, subprocess
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+tracked = subprocess.check_output(['git', 'ls-files', '-z'], cwd=ROOT).decode('utf8').split('\0')
+initial = dict(
+    head=subprocess.check_output(['git', 'rev-parse', 'HEAD'], cwd=ROOT).decode().strip(),
+    tracked_sha256={p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT/p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in tracked if p},
+    status=subprocess.check_output(['git', 'status', '--porcelain'], cwd=ROOT).decode(),
+    db_sha256=hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
+)
+assert not (OUT/'integrity_before.json').exists(), 'Do not overwrite initial snapshot'
+(OUT/'integrity_before.json').write_text(json.dumps(initial, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
+manifest = []
+for sub in ['root_checks', 'cost']:
+    dest = OUT/sub
+    (dest/'tmp').mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+    for src in sorted((ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m14'/sub).glob('*.py')):
+        code = src.read_text(encoding='utf8')
+        changes = []
+        for old, new in [('audit_evidence_claims/m14/', 'audit_evidence_claims/m15/')]:
+            if old in code:
+                code = code.replace(old, new)
+                changes.append(f'{old} -> {new}')
+        (dest/src.name).write_text(code, encoding='utf8')
+        manifest.append(dict(source=src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(), copy=(dest/src.name).relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(), sha256=hashlib.sha256(src.read_bytes()).hexdigest(), changes=changes, output_root='__file__ relative m15; exact historical case inputs retained'))
+src = ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m14/finalize_integrity.py'
+(OUT/'finalize_integrity.py').write_text(src.read_text(encoding='utf8').replace('8952]', '8952,8945]'), encoding='utf8')
+(OUT/'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding='utf8')
+print(json.dumps(dict(head=initial['head'], tracked=len(initial['tracked_sha256']), scripts=len(manifest))))
